The dashboard's `/api/factory-reset` (and every other API) was reachable by anonymous LAN attackers given any LAN-binding misconfiguration; the P0.0.1-era S2 tripwire kept the deferral honest, but the substantive fix is shipped here. **D1 — token generation + corruption recovery + Windows ACL** (`core/dashboard_token.py` NEW ~250 LOC): `_VALID_TOKEN_RE = ^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{43}$` shape lock; `_atomic_write_secret` writes `.tmp.<pid>` + fsync + chmod-on-tmp + os.replace (canonical file never world-readable); `_apply_windows_acl` invokes icacls with the locked argv shape `['icacls', path, '/inheritance:r', '/grant:r', f'{user}:F']` + `check=False` + `capture_output=True` + `timeout=5.0` (subprocess function-scope re-import per Plan v2 Q5-C string-form patch resilience); `_ensure_dashboard_token` is the pipeline-boot entry point — cleanup partial writes → existence check → shape-validate → on-corrupt backup `.dashboard_token.corrupt.<unix_ts>` + regenerate + WARNING log + new auth URL → on-valid self-heal mode drift via `_verify_mode_self_heal`. Plan v2 §3.13 corruption-recovery design: `.corrupt.<ts>` suffix preserves forensic evidence + excludes file from future regeneration check + self-heal-by-default since pipeline boot doesn't block on dashboard state (cookies tied to the previous token need re-auth — accepted UX cost over refusing-to-boot). Boot integration: first line of `pipeline.py::run()` before any other boot work. `core/db.py::wipe_all()` carries a verbatim 8-line preservation comment block naming the invariant (re-issuing auth URL on every factory reset is hostile UX + single-user scope = no cross-tenant risk). **D2 — `/api/auth` route** (`dog-ai-dashboard/app/api/auth/route.ts` NEW): GET handler with `?token=<43-char>` validation via `crypto.timingSafeEqual` (length-equality pre-check; bare `===` would short-circuit on first differing byte → timing-attack surface); cookie attributes locked at `{name: 'dogai_session', httpOnly: true, sameSite: 'strict', path: '/', maxAge: 315360000}` (10-year TTL — single-user localhost rotation is via token replacement, not TTL); on success 302 redirect to `/` + `fs.unlinkSync(AUTH_URL_PATH)` wrapped in try/catch for Plan v2 §3.12 double-click idempotency (second click of consumed URL: validation still succeeds against `.dashboard_token`, unlink no-ops on ENOENT, harmless re-issued Set-Cookie); validation reads ONLY from `.dashboard_token` source-of-truth — `.dashboard_auth_url` deletion is hygiene, NOT validation logic (P4 invariant). Distinguishable error bodies: 400 missing param, 401 mismatch ("Unauthorized"), 401 ENOENT ("Pipeline not started — token not yet generated") so operator can diagnose. **D3 — middleware coverage** (`dog-ai-dashboard/middleware.ts` NEW): matcher `'/api/:path*'` covers every `/api/*` by default; inline allowlist for `/api/auth` is the ONLY carve-out (would create chicken/egg gating the route that mints cookies); per-request `fs.readFileSync(_tokenPath(), 'utf8')` — NO module-scope token cache so manual token replacement / corruption recovery is reflected without server restart; cookie via `req.cookies.get('dogai_session')`; length-prechecked `crypto.timingSafeEqual` mirrors the auth-route helper. The motivating-failure regression guard `test_factory_reset_is_gated_by_middleware` confirms the entire P0.S2 motivation — anonymous attacker can no longer wipe via `/api/factory-reset`. **D4 — file-read invariant**: structural check on both middleware.ts (no module-scope cache) AND `_ensure_dashboard_token` (re-reads file every call); behavioral check that missing token → 401 with distinguishable body. **D5 — bind invariant + launch wrapper** (`dog-ai-dashboard/scripts/launch.js` NEW ~140 LOC): `DEFAULT_BIND='127.0.0.1'` + `_resolveBind()` reads `DASHBOARD_BIND` env var; argv-injection regex `VALID_BIND_RE = /^[a-zA-Z0-9.:_-]+$/` fires BEFORE the 0.0.0.0 double-opt-in check (defense against `DASHBOARD_BIND="0.0.0.0 --foo bar"`-style injection that would otherwise pass simple equality); 0.0.0.0 (bind-all-interfaces) requires `DASHBOARD_BIND_ALLOW_ANY=1` second-opt-in env var with hard-error stderr naming the variable + threat ("ALL interfaces, exposing the dashboard to every network the host is reachable on"); 6-line WARNING block emits on every non-localhost bind; `--dry-run` flag prints resolved argv to stdout + exits 0 (testable surface); `child_process.spawn` with `stdio: 'inherit'` + `shell: process.platform === 'win32'` for the .cmd shim. `package.json` scripts now invoke `node scripts/launch.js dev|start` instead of inlining `--hostname` flags. **D6 — factory-reset preservation**: P0.S2 preservation comment block at BOTH `core/db.py::wipe_all` AND `dog-ai-dashboard/app/api/factory-reset/route.ts` (inline `// NOTE:` reinforcement at end of array body — defense-in-depth against future array-additions). Naming the invariant in both Python and Node mirrors the discipline that any `.dashboard_token` or `.dashboard_auth_url` deletion in either path is a regression. **D7 — mode + ACL enforcement**: POSIX path uses os.chmod(0o600); Windows path adds icacls via `_apply_windows_acl`; both run on every boot via `_verify_mode_self_heal` (idempotent re-icacls on Windows; POSIX detects drift + WARNs + restores). icacls failures (FileNotFoundError / TimeoutExpired / returncode≠0) all WARN-and-continue per Plan v1 §1.P3 "best-effort + warn user" — pipeline never crashes on Windows ACL quirks. **S2 tripwire REFACTORED per Plan v1 §2 test 21**: old `test_dashboard_package_json_scripts_explicitly_bind_localhost` (asserting `--hostname 127.0.0.1` literal in package.json) replaced by `test_dashboard_package_json_scripts_invoke_launch_wrapper` (scripts MUST start with `node ` + contain `scripts/launch.js`) + NEW `test_dashboard_launch_script_enforces_default_localhost` (wrapper source has DEFAULT_BIND in locked localhost set + DASHBOARD_BIND env-var branch + DASHBOARD_BIND_ALLOW_ANY double-opt-in branch + 0.0.0.0 mention). Same invariant ("default bind localhost; LAN opt-out requires explicit env-var"); new surface (logic moved from package.json to launch.js wrapper). **36 collected tests** (`tests/test_dashboard_token.py` 12 + `tests/test_dashboard_token_windows.py` 2 + `tests/test_dashboard_middleware.py` 7 + `tests/test_dashboard_auth_route.py` 8 + `tests/test_dashboard_launch_script.py` 6 + `tests/test_dashboard_bind_tripwire.py` +1 new): 2 POSIX-only D7 tests skip on Windows dev machine (34 passed; 6 total skipped including 2 P0.S7.5.2 carryover). **Defense-in-depth classification per Plan v2 §11 operational definition LOCKED at closure**: 10 DiD tests (atomic-replace, corruption-recovery + complement, `_verify_token_shape` unit pair, icacls failure recovery, middleware ENOENT→401, auth missing-param→400, constant-time-compare structural, launch double-opt-in success, launch argv-injection rejection) + 26 behavioral-correctness tests. **Plan v2 §11 verbatim operational definition** banked in `feedback_auditor_q5_estimates_trail_grep.md`: "Defense-in-depth test = verifies behavior under input that has NOT been observed in production AND would NOT be caught by the motivating-failure regression guard. The 2026-05-20 multi-person live canary surfaced 5 independent root causes across the reconciler / pipeline / voice gallery / audio / brain prompt-blocks: (1) Jagan's offscreen-floor false-fire on legitimate-but-short utterances; (2) Lexi's voice-routing engagement-gate never firing because the new_stranger path didn't mirror ambient-gate semantics; (3) voice gallery centroid contamination from short-utterance noise; (4) Whisper 1-word artifacts ("You", "Yeah", "Thank") triggering phantom turns; (5) brain mis-tooling canary-3 question-shape ("Do you know me?") to `report_identity_mismatch` on stranger turn 1. **D1 (P0 CORRECTNESS)** — `_rs_pif_view` producer at `pipeline.py:7234` now mirrors the full `PresenceSnapshot` schema including `last_recognized_at`; reconciler's offscreen-floor predicate stops false-firing because the dict no longer falls back to `.get(..., 0)`. Producer fix per auditor lean (D1.b) — NOT consumer change — preserves the semantic distinction between "face recognized within X" vs "any-source presence touched within X" (voice-source entries still excluded by `last_recognized_at=0.0` AND `source != "voice"` defense-in-depth). **D2 (P0 CORRECTNESS)** — voice-routing new_stranger branch at `pipeline.py:7496-7515` mirrors the ambient-gate engagement semantics via NFKC-lowercased substring match (`_nfkc_lower(_system_name) in _nfkc_lower(text)`); when stranger says system name in turn 1's STT, the session opens with `engagement_gate_passed=True` + `set_waiting_for_name(False)` + `set_voice_only_origin(True)` matching the ambient path. Without this, voice-routing-opened sessions stay stuck at `bootstrap_credits=0` forever. **D3 (MEDIUM)** — voice gallery accumulation gates added: minimum-utterance-duration (`MIN_VOICE_ACCUM_DURATION_SECS=1.5`s — matches ECAPA-TDNN min reliable length per `core/voice.py:147`) gate at `pipeline.py::_accumulate_voice` call site + centroid-distance (`VOICE_SELF_UPDATE_CENTROID_MIN=0.55` — mirrors face-gallery's Session 51 design) gate inside `core/db.py::add_voice_embedding`. Bootstrap-safe (`VOICE_CENTROID_GATE_MIN_SAMPLES=5`): centroid gate inactive until gallery has ≥5 samples. Plan v2 §3.1 LOCKED recompute (NOT cache) — cache-invalidation would require enumerating 6+ gallery-write sites + AST-asserting per P0.5 inverse-check discipline; not worth ~5ms saved on a rare event. Brain kept seeing the unknown-branch VISITOR CONTEXT template ("Someone stopped by but didn't tell me their name…") and followed it faithfully into "No one was here" fabrication on Jagan's return-asks. **D1 fix at `core/brain_agent.py:3038-3063`**: replaced literal-substring check with `re.sub(r"\[visitor_name:[^\]]+\]", lambda _m: new_marker, content)`. Wildcard pattern matches ANY current placeholder (`unknown` / real name / future drift); **lambda replacement** (callable, not f-string) means `new_marker` is used VERBATIM — no regex backreference interpretation of `new_name` (`\1`, `\g<name>`, `\\`). Defense-in-depth Gate 2 upgraded from N/A → ✓ per Plan v2 §6 (closes the regex-backreference-injection-via-name vector — relevant if future LLM-hallucinated names contain escape sequences). **6 tests added across 2 phases** in new `tests/test_p0_s7_5_1.py`: Phase 1 (5) — `test_regex_swaps_existing_marker` (unit primary happy path), `test_regex_noop_when_marker_absent` (unit defensive), `test_regex_idempotent_when_already_renamed` (unit), `test_update_alert_uses_regex_not_literal_substring` (AST forward-property — locates `update_visitor_alert_for_promoted_person` via AST walk + asserts `re.sub` call exists AND no `.replace(` with `[visitor_name:` f-string arg; structural over source-inspection because docstrings can contain the literal-substring form even when actual call is gone — 4th instance of `feedback_adjacent_string_literal_normalizer.md` pattern), `test_regex_replacement_handles_backslash_in_new_name` (Plan v2 §2.5 defense-in-depth — `new_name="Test\\name"` round-trips byte-identically; if implementation reverted to f-string, `\n` would be interpreted). Phase 2 (1) — `test_visitor_alert_marker_swap_e2e` (full lifecycle: seed stranger + best_friend + turn → `_run_visitor_alert` queues with `[visitor_name:unknown]` → rename stranger to Lexi in faces.db → `on_identity_confirmed` fires → assert marker swapped to `[visitor_name:Lexi]` + metadata reconciled). **1/1 deliberate-regression confirmation passed** (a): reverted lambda → f-string replacement; test 4b (`test_regex_replacement_handles_backslash_in_new_name`) failed correctly — `re.sub` interpreted `\n` in `Test\name` as a literal newline character chr(10), so content rendered as `[visitor_name:Test<NL>ame]` (newline) NOT `[visitor_name:Test\name]` (literal backslash). P0.S7.5.1 D1 fixes the marker-update path going forward; it does NOT migrate existing broken nudges in production databases. Specifically: nudges queued BEFORE D1 deployment with `[visitor_name:unknown]` content where the visitor was later promoted to a real name (Lexi etc.) will retain the broken `[visitor_name:unknown]` marker until their natural `expires_at` (24h after queue) OR manual dismissal. Brain will continue surfacing the unknown-branch VISITOR CONTEXT template ("Someone stopped by but didn't tell me their name...") for these specific nudges. New nudges queued AFTER D1 deployment work correctly: regex swap fires at promotion time, marker reconciles with metadata, brain surfaces the visitor by name. Rationale for no migration: factory-reset between canaries is the project's canary discipline; canary 3 starts with clean state. In production, broken nudges expire naturally within 24h. Migration script would add complexity for a transient liability with a known TTL. When to revisit: if production runs without factory-reset between canaries become normal practice, OR if the 24h expiry proves too long for stale-nudge user experience, file a follow-up spec to add a one-shot migration. RENAME` in `_m_*_apply` bodies, with single documented exemption (`_m_0010_drop_conversation_memory_apply` — S24 legacy cleanup); (4) sanity guard that the exemption's body actually contains the destructive op (catches typos in DOCUMENTED_EXEMPTIONS set). **Boot observability** added: `[Schema] {db_label}: bootstrap stamped baseline v=1 + N pre-existing migration(s) as is_initial=1 (legacy DB)` / `apply_migrations ran 0 pending` / `ledger already versioned` lines fire on every boot — Jagan's validation gate consumed these.

**P0.9 explicit non-claims** (per Plan v2 exit gate item #5):
- **Additive-only**: P0.9 enforces no destructive schema changes outside the single documented S24 DROP TABLE exemption. If a future migration genuinely needs a destructive op (table rename, column drop, type change), it requires explicit DOCUMENTED_EXEMPTIONS extension AND a reversibility decision — not silently allowed via "well it's just a migration."
- **FK enforcement is separate**: P0.9 versioned migrations do NOT add `PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON` to faces.db or brain.db. Only classifier_scenarios.db enforces FKs (Spec 1 inherited this). Adding FK enforcement to the other two DBs is a separate behavior decision (would break embeddings/voice_embeddings rows with stranger person_ids that get pruned).
- **Cross-DB invariants unenforced**: P0.9 versions each DB's schema independently. There is no ledger that records "faces.db migration v=12 must land before brain.db migration v=7" — cross-DB ordering invariants (e.g. brain.db reads a faces.db column the migration just added) are still developer-discipline-only.

| **P0.9.2 COMPLETE** (Phase 2 of 3 — retrofit; Phase 3 cleans up redundant inline calls). 19 historical schema mutations across faces.db (10 migrations: v=2 through v=10) and brain.db (10 migrations: v=2 through v=11) retrofitted into versioned `MIGRATIONS` lists with full 5-tuple shape (apply_fn / verify_post_fn / verify_present_fn). classifier_scenarios.db kept its existing v=1/v=2 Spec 1 ledger entries (no retrofit needed — already versioned). **Migration type alias extended to 5-tuple** — the two verify companions encode genuinely different roles: `verify_post` raises if the post-state is wrong (used by the runner after apply_fn), `verify_present` returns bool for the bootstrap predicate. The split is load-bearing for data backfills (S107 P3A.6 conversation_log + S95 P3A.4 privacy_level remediation), where verify_post must check "backfill complete" while verify_present checks "column exists" — conflating them would let bootstrap stamp `is_initial=1` on a partially-backfilled DB and the runner would never finish the job. **`bootstrap_ledger_if_unversioned` extended with MIGRATIONS walk** — after stamping the v=1 baseline on a legacy DB, walks the provided MIGRATIONS list, calls `verify_present(conn)` per entry, stamps `is_initial=1` on any whose artifact is already on disk. Callers (7+ extraction agents) do `parsed.get(...)` assuming dict → silent `AttributeError` at runtime on any non-dict valid JSON. Falsifying input from `TestArbitraryText` was `raw='0'`. Fix: added `return result if isinstance(result, dict) else None` gate; also broadened except clauses to catch `RecursionError` (deeply-nested DoS) and `ValueError` (Python 3.11+ integer-string conversion DoS — see Bug #2). **Bug #2 (`core/brain.py::_parse_intent_sidecar` uncaught ValueError)**: input with a 5000-digit integer literal triggered Python 3.11+'s `sys.get_int_max_str_digits()=4300` DoS limit, raising `ValueError` (not `JSONDecodeError`), which the original except clause caught exclusively. Adversarial LLM output could crash the parser. Falsifying input from `TestLargeInput` was `payload='1'` (Hypothesis-simplified to a long run of '1's exceeding the limit). Fix: extended except clauses on both code paths to `(json.JSONDecodeError, RecursionError, ValueError)`. **+33 tests** in `tests/test_brain_json_parser_hypothesis.py` (Hypothesis property-based, `max_examples=1000`/test per P0.12 spec). Failure-mode taxonomy covered: arbitrary text, truncated streams (sampled-prefix + random-tail + random-cutoff), code-block fences (markdown ```json wrapping), doubled keys (Python json contract: last wins — pinned as regression guard), unescaped nested quotes, trailing commas, BOM + leading whitespace, surrogate / control / format unicode, empty / whitespace-only, extremely large input (1 MB repeated payload + N-key dicts up to 5000), deeply nested (`[`×N + `]`×N AND `{"a":` ×N — up to 2000 depth), round-trip soundness (any JSON-serializable Python value → `json.dumps` → `_parse_json` round-trips correctly when top-level dict), explicit failure-mode seeds (14 hand-picked pathologies including `"\x00\x01\x02"` raw control chars and `{"x": NaN}` JSON extension). **Hard regression tests** for the two production fixes pin the exact falsifying inputs: `test_bug1_parse_json_returns_none_for_non_dict_top_level`, `test_bug2_parse_intent_sidecar_handles_oversized_int_string`, `test_bug2_parse_json_also_handles_oversized_int`, plus a source-inspection guard `test_bug1_fix_visible_in_source` that catches future reverts via `inspect.getsource` + AST-style string check on the dict-isinstance gate. **CI cost**: full Hypothesis suite runs in 12s on this machine — under the 60s budget the P0.12 spec allowed. **Architectural pattern reinforced**: induction surfaces invariant gaps. Hypothesis dep added: `hypothesis==6.152.7`. | **P0.11 COMPLETE** — preventive hardening for `core.state._persistent` dict race. **Framing**: the race has NEVER been observed in production. The single writer (`pipeline.py:6264 state.set_persistent("anti_spoof_enabled", _)`) runs ONCE at startup, before the event loop, before any reader can run. P0.11 closes the door against THREE latent activation conditions: (1) a future runtime `set_persistent` call lands after startup, (2) `state.write()` is moved off the asyncio loop into an executor thread, (3) `state.write()` gains an `await` point and the writer interleaves between `**_persistent` spread and JSON dump. **Production change** (`core/state.py:16-31`): replaced `_persistent[key] = value` (in-place subscript-mutation) with atomic-replace pattern — `global _persistent; _persistent = {**_persistent, key: value}`. CPython `STORE_NAME` is GIL-atomic, so concurrent readers holding the OLD dict reference see a consistent snapshot; the new dict only becomes visible on next deref. Docstring is honest about scope: protects readers from torn iteration, does NOT protect against concurrent writers losing updates (RMW race — multi-writer correctness requires explicit `threading.Lock`, deferred until runtime writers land). **+4 tests** in `tests/test_state_race.py`: (1) **behavioral race** — 1 writer thread does 1000 sequential `set_persistent` calls while 1000 reader threads spam `state.write()`; asserts zero `RuntimeError("dictionary changed size during iteration")` + final dict contains all 1000 keys (single-writer guarantee per P1 polish); (2) **torn-state probe** — producer cycles `(k1=v1)` then `(k1=v1, k2=v2)` while 8 reader threads probe; asserts reader never observes `{k2: v2}` without `k1` (which would only materialize if a half-built dict leaked across the GIL); (3) **AST repo-wide ban** on `_persistent[X] = Y` subscript-assign in `pipeline.py` + `core/*.py` — detector catches BOTH bare-name (`_persistent[X]=Y`, in-module) AND attribute-form (`state._persistent[X]=Y`, external-callers); (4) **AST guard** that every `_persistent = ...` rebind in `core/state.py` is accompanied by a `global _persistent` declaration in the same function body (caught when Regression #2 dropped the global decl → assignment would silently create a function-local). AST scopes documented per P2 polish: subscript-assign detector covers `Assign/AugAssign/AnnAssign` direct subscripts (not `.update()` mutators or destructuring); rebind detector covers `ast.Assign` direct targets (not AugAssign/destructuring/walrus — vanishingly rare for module globals). **Deliberate-regression checks (×3, all confirmed)**: (1) reverted `set_persistent` to `_persistent[key]=value` → Test #3 fired with `core/state.py:30: subscript-assign forbidden`; (2) dropped `global _persistent` decl → Test #4 fired with `function 'set_persistent' rebinds _persistent without declaring global`; (3) injected `state._persistent["regression_check_3"]=True` in `pipeline.py:6265` → Test #3 caught attribute-form violation outside state.py. All three reverted; suite green. Without the checkpoint, `wait_for` cancellation cannot fire mid-loop and transaction rollback never runs — partial SQL writes commit durably despite timeout firing. P0.8.1's `TestHardCaseCancellationRollback` proved the property structurally when checkpoints exist; F1 ENFORCES they continue to exist as the codebase grows. **F2 — retry-path one-shot guarantee**: `ask_retry_text` MUST internally call `_stream_together_raw(..., include_tools=False)` on every code path. AST scan walks the function body, finds every `_stream_together_raw` invocation, asserts each carries `include_tools=False` as a literal `False` constant (no kwarg / non-literal / `True` all fail). Sync-mutator allowlist regex widened to permit bare `peek` (matches the canonical store-pattern read verb). **P0.6.7 (v2) closure** locks the migration as a permanent structural invariant: (1) ratchet test `tests/test_p06_legacy_global_progress.py` runs every PR, all 28 globals at cap=0; (2) `tests/test_p06_store_schemas.py` pins `EXPECTED_FIELDS` per Store with 15 schema tests — drift in any owned-field set fails CI; (3) `tests/test_p06_store_inverse_checks.py` enforces paired-write discipline with 19 AST-based inverse checks — cloud-bundle (4-field atomic), room-triple-tuple (3-field atomic), VoiceGalleryStore (gallery, sizes) pair, VisionFrameStore (frame, frame_time) pair, plus per-field writer enumeration for the simpler stores; (4) M2 autouse meta-test verifies both conftest files reset all 9 stores (8 P0.6 + 1 P0.7 SessionStore); (5) producer-copy AST source-inspection test (`test_vision_frame_store_producer_copy.py`) scans every `set_frame(...)` call site in pipeline.py and asserts `.copy()` in the same expression; (6) shim sweep confirmed clean — zero P0.6 migration scaffolding remains. `_sync_set_cloud_state` / `_sync_mint_room` / `_sync_add_room_participant` / `_sync_clear_room` / `_sync_set_prev_det_count` documented as load-bearing public sync API (NOT shims).
